While companies clamor to bring you the most efficient internet access, the most simplified controls, and the most intuitive displays, much of their success depends on the effective fusion of form and function. From the simple honeycomb pattern that made the Nest Protect sensor an early standout to the innovative Google router that is just too alluring to be stashed under the sofa and out of sight, the best smart products are often (we’d like to think not coincidentally) some of the best-looking.\nGoogle OnHub For its first-ever router, the design team at Google upended our traditional notions of the device as something that should be hidden from view. Working off the knowledge that a router out in the open will achieve much better connectivity, they set about creating a more aesthetically pleasing one, even partnering with artists to conceive one-of-a-kind covers for the devices; $200 each. google.com\nNetatmo Welcome The French company released its sleek, brushed-bronze security camera last year to much acclaim. Besides looking great, the system incorporates advanced face-recognition technology to alert you when certain members of your family arrive home. When the system hears an alarm (entrance, smoke, or carbon monoxide), it alerts you and automatically records a video; $199. netatmo.com\nEero Router CEO Nick Weaver founded Eero when he became fed up with a worsening Wi-Fi connection after moving into a larger home. The concept: multiple routers connected to one network, effectively spreading your connectivity farther. The routers are smartphone-controlled, allowing you to set time limits, create unique passwords, and monitor access, all with the swipe of a finger. The compact, minimalist devices are pretty enough to be left out, blending seamlessly into a white space; from $199 for a single device. eero.com\nNest Learning Thermostat The sleek thermostat from early smart-home purveyor Nest is a case study in form and function working in tandem. Its round screen lights up in different colors to signify various settings and warnings (orange for heating, blue for cooling, black for an empty home), making it an intuitive system that’s easy to work into your life; $249. nest.com\nPlume Pod The Plume’s looks are deceiving. The device stores router technology in a cloud, so the only physical aspect is a small “pod” that gets plugged into your outlet. Such a setup also allows the software to continually self-optimize to bring you the best connection. The tiny device, which starts shipping this fall, comes in onyx, silver, and Champagne faceted designs; $39 per pod. plumewifi.com\nAmazon Dot After the wildly successful introduction of Amazon’s Echo and its Alexa technology, the tech giant will release this fall the Dot, a tiny round device that connects to any speaker and essentially makes it an Echo. So if you’re an audiophile who is intrigued by the Echo’s capability but will never change your Sonos system, the Dot solves both problems. Compact, efficient, and easy to use, it’s ideal for both the tech-savvy and the smarthome uninitiated; $90.           </script><script type="text/javascript" data-testid="growthbook-script">(function (props) {
        const { userAuthenticated, section, contentType, hasBuyButtons, clientSdkKey, apiHost, enableClient, brandSlug } = props;
        const CookieAttributes = {
            AMG_USER_PARTNER: 'amg_user_partner',
            CN_XID: 'CN_xid',
            CN_SEGMENTS: 'CN_segments',
            CN_GROUPS: 'CN_groups',
            CN_GEO_COUNTRY_CODE: 'CN_geo_country_code',
            CN_DEBUG: 'cn-debug'
        };
        function extractExperimentData(experiment, result) {
            return {
                experiment_id: experiment.key,
                feature_id: result.featureId || '',
                variation: result.key,
                variation_name: result.name || '',
                payload: result.value
            };
        }
        function updateDataLayer(expDataLayer) {
            if (!Array.isArray(window.dataLayer) ||
                typeof window.dataLayer[0] !== 'object' ||
                expDataLayer.length === 0) {
                return;
            }
            const mergeAssignments = (newAssignments = [], existingAssignments = []) => {
                const merged = [...existingAssignments];
                newAssignments.forEach((newAssignment) => {
                    const existingIndex = merged.findIndex((existing) => existing.experiment_id === newAssignment.experiment_id);
                    if (existingIndex >= 0) {
                        merged[existingIndex] = newAssignment;
                    }
                    else {
                        merged.push(newAssignment);
                    }
                });
                return merged;
            };
            window.dataLayer[0].experiments = mergeAssignments(expDataLayer, window.dataLayer[0].experiments || []);
            if (window.cns) {
                const { experiments } = window.dataLayer[0];
                const { pageContext } = window.cns;
                if (experiments && experiments.length) {
                    pageContext.experiments = formatExperimentsForPageContext(experiments);
                }
            }
            const kendraGrowthBook = window.Kendra.GROWTHBOOK;
            if (kendraGrowthBook && kendraGrowthBook.broadcast) {
                kendraGrowthBook.broadcast('experiments.assignments.processed');
            }
        }
        function formatExperimentsForPageContext(experiments) {
            if (!experiments.length) {
                return {};
            }
            const bucketLabel = [];
            const experimentLabel = [];
            const payloadArray = [];
            experiments.forEach((experiment) => {
                const { variation_name, experiment_id, payload } = experiment;
                bucketLabel.push(`${variation_name}`);
                experimentLabel.push(`${experiment_id}`);
                if (Array.isArray(payload)) {
                    payloadArray.push(...payload);
                }
                else if (payload) {
                    payloadArray.push(payload);
                }
            });
            return {
                bucketLabel,
                experimentLabel,
                payload: payloadArray
            };
        }
        function perfMark(name) {
            if (typeof window.performance !== 'undefined' &&
                typeof window.performance.mark !== 'undefined') {
                window.performance.mark(name);
            }
        }
        function perfMeasure(name, startMark, endMark) {
            if (typeof window.performance !== 'undefined' &&
                typeof window.performance.measure !== 'undefined') {
                try {
                    window.performance.measure(name, startMark, endMark);
                }
                catch (e) {
                    console.error(`Error setting performance measure: ${e}`);
                }
            }
        }
        function getCookie(name) {
            if (typeof document === 'undefined' || !name) {
                return '';
            }
            const cookiePieces = document.cookie.split(/;\s?/);
            let cookieValue = '';
            for (let i = 0; i < cookiePieces.length; i++) {
                const parts = cookiePieces[i].split('=');
                if (parts[0] === name) {
                    cookieValue = decodeURIComponent(parts.slice(1).join('='));
                    break;
                }
            }
            return cookieValue;
        }
        /**
         * Decode CN_groups JWT and extract user groups
         * Supports both v1 (array of {id}) and v2 (object with category arrays) structures
         */
        function getUserGroupsFromJwt() {
            try {
                const token = getCookie(CookieAttributes.CN_GROUPS);
                if (!token)
                    return [];
                const parts = token.split('.');
                if (parts.length !== 3)
                    return [];
                // Decode header to get version
                const header = JSON.parse(atob(parts[0].replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/')));
                const ver = header?.ver;
                // Decode payload
                const payload = JSON.parse(atob(parts[1].replace(/-/g, '+').replace(/_/g, '/')));
                const groups = payload?.groups;
                if (!groups)
                    return [];
                let ids = [];
                if (ver === 1) {
                    // v1: groups is array of {id, rules}
                    if (Array.isArray(groups)) {
                        ids = groups
                            .map((g) => g?.id)
                            .filter((id) => typeof id === 'string');
                    }
                }
                else if (ver === 2) {
                    // v2: groups is object with category keys (ds, td, referral, etc.)
                    if (typeof groups === 'object' && !Array.isArray(groups)) {
                        ids = Object.values(groups)
                            .filter(Array.isArray)
                            .flat()
                            .filter((id) => typeof id === 'string');
                    }
                }
                return ids;
            }
            catch (e) {
                console.error('Error decoding CN_groups JWT:', e);
                return [];
            }
        }
        const isDesktopBrowser = () => {
            const { userAgent } = navigator;
            return !/Android|webOS|iPhone|iPad|iPod|BlackBerry|IEMobile|Opera Mini/i.test(userAgent);
        };
        const localStorageXidCleanUp = (prefix, xid) => {
            if (!xid) {
                return;
            }
            for (let i = 0; i < localStorage.length; i++) {
                const key = window.localStorage.key(i);
                if (key && key.startsWith(prefix) && !key.includes(xid)) {
                    window.localStorage.removeItem(key);
                }
            }
        };
        const isMobile = !isDesktopBrowser();
        const userXid = getCookie(CookieAttributes.CN_XID);
        try {
            const segment = getCookie(CookieAttributes.CN_SEGMENTS) || null;
            const userGroups = getUserGroupsFromJwt();
            const amgUserPartner = getCookie(CookieAttributes.AMG_USER_PARTNER) || '';
            const countryCode = getCookie(CookieAttributes.CN_GEO_COUNTRY_CODE) || '';
            window.growthbook_config = window.growthbook_config || {};
            const { userAgent } = navigator;
            window.growthbook_config.attributes = {
                userAgent,
                id: userXid,
                url: window.location.href,
                height: window.innerHeight,
                width: window.innerWidth,
                isMobile,
                loggedIn: userAuthenticated,
                segment,
                userGroups,
                section,
                contentType,
                hasBuyButtons,
                amgUserPartner,
                countryCode,
                brandSlug
            };
            window.growthbook_config.trackingCallback = (experiment, result) => {
                const expDataLayer = [];
                const experimentData = extractExperimentData(experiment, result);
                const currentExp = {
                    ...experimentData,
                    status: 'CLIENT_ASSIGNMENT'
                };
                expDataLayer.push(currentExp);
                updateDataLayer(expDataLayer);
                perfMark(`gb-script-tracking-end`);
                perfMeasure(`gb-script-tracking-finish`, `gb-script-end`, `gb-script-tracking-end`);
            };
            window.growthbook_config.noStreaming = true;
            // The SDK drains this queue after `growthbook.min.js` initializes a client instance.
            window.growthbook_queue = window.growthbook_queue || [];
            window.growthbook_queue.push((gb) => {
                const markReady = function () {
                    perfMark('gb-script-end');
                    perfMeasure('gb-script-ready', 'gb-script-start', 'gb-script-end');
                };
                if (gb.ready) {
                    markReady();
                    return;
                }
                // `growthbookdata` fires once the SDK finishes loading data, at which point gb.ready should be true.
                const onGrowthbookData = function () {
                    if (gb.ready) {
                        document.removeEventListener('growthbookdata', onGrowthbookData);
                        markReady();
                    }
                };
                document.addEventListener('growthbookdata', onGrowthbookData);
            });
        }
        catch (e) {
            console.error('GrowthBook config setup error:', e);
        }
        try {
            const stickyBrands = ['tatler', 'woi'];
            const isStickyBucketingEnabled = stickyBrands.includes(brandSlug) && !!userXid;
            if (isStickyBucketingEnabled) {
                localStorageXidCleanUp('gbStickyBuckets__', userXid);
            }
            if (clientSdkKey && apiHost && enableClient && userXid) {
                const cnDebug = getCookie(CookieAttributes.CN_DEBUG) || false;
                const s = document.createElement('script');
                s.async = true;
                s.dataset.clientKey = clientSdkKey;
                s.dataset.apiHost = apiHost;
                if (isStickyBucketingEnabled) {
                    s.dataset.useStickyBucketService = 'localStorage';
                    s.dataset.stickyBucketPrefix = 'gbStickyBuckets__';
                }
                s.dataset.uuid = userXid;
                s.dataset.enableDevMode = cnDebug.toString();
                s.src = `/verso/static/assets/growthbook.min.js`;
                document.head.appendChild(s);
                perfMark(`gb-script-start`);
            }
            else {
                console.info('SDK loading skipped - missing requirements:', {
                    hasClientSdkKey: !!clientSdkKey,
                    hasApiHost: !!apiHost,
                    enableClient,
                    hasExpId: !!userXid
                });
            }
        }
        catch (e) {
            console.error('GrowthBook SDK loading error:', e);
        }

AssetEmbedCaption-eZIMNW gMgneI asset-embed__caption" data-testid="caption-wrapper"><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionText-cQpRdU gNMWmI hbiMYj caption__text"><p>The Netatmo Welcome is one of a slew of smart home devices that actually looks good in the home.</p>
</span><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionCredit-cUgOGk gwmweI hRFzlA caption__credit">Courtesy of Netatmo</span></div></figure><p>As cutting-edge as the <a href="https://www.architecturaldigest.com/story/how-to-set-up-smart-home" class="text link">smart-home</a> movement may seem, the best products to come out of the connected craze so far hark back to the age-old fundamental of good design. While companies clamor to bring you the most efficient internet access, the most simplified controls, and the most intuitive displays, much of their success depends on the effective fusion of form and function. From the simple honeycomb pattern that made the Nest Protect sensor an early standout to the innovative Google router that is just too alluring to be stashed under the sofa and out of sight, the best smart products are often (we’d like to think not coincidentally) some of the best-looking.</p><figure class="AssetEmbedWrapper-iJvQnD cOWUYC asset-embed"><div class="AssetEmbedAssetContainer-fnduJP iaVSwI asset-embed__asset-container"><span class="SpanWrapper-kFnjvc eKnjjD responsive-asset AssetEmbedResponsiveAsset-gaAbQ hXaxHA asset-embed__responsive-asset"><picture various artists." loading="lazy" class="ResponsiveImageContainer-dkeESL cQPiWi responsive-image__image" AssetEmbedCaption-eZIMNW gMgneI asset-embed__caption" data-testid="caption-wrapper"><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionText-cQpRdU gNMWmI hbiMYj caption__text"><p>Shells for Google’s OnHub wireless router designed by various artists.</p>
</span><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionCredit-cUgOGk gwmweI hRFzlA caption__credit">Courtesy of Google</span></div></figure></div></div></div><div class="GridItem-bufwYC fdedvq grid--item grid-layout__aside"></div></div><div class="GridWrapper-uulHE dCnZHU grid grid-margins grid-items-2 ArticlePageChunksGrid-kSknMg dSvDdx grid-layout--adrail narrow wide-adrail" data-journey-hook="grid-wrapper"><div class="GridItem-bufwYC kKTABR grid--item grid-layout__content"><div class="BodyWrapper-cuqCAc fFudnb body body__container article__body" data-journey-hook="client-content" data-testid="BodyWrapper"><div class="body__inner-container"><p><strong>Google OnHub</strong> For its first-ever router, the design team at Google upended our traditional notions of the device as something that should be hidden from view. Working off the knowledge that a router out in the open will achieve much better connectivity, they set about creating a more aesthetically pleasing one, even partnering with artists to conceive <a href="https://www.architecturaldigest.com/story/google-onhub-router" class="text link">one-of-a-kind covers</a> for the devices; $200 each. <a href="https://on.google.com/hub/" class="text link"><em>google.com</em></a></p><div class="AdWrapper-fGiLaV iGDxYx ad ad--in-content"><div class="ad__slot ad__slot--in-content" data-node-id="yuassd"></div></div><figure AssetEmbedCaption-eZIMNW gMgneI asset-embed__caption" data-testid="caption-wrapper"><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionText-cQpRdU gNMWmI hbiMYj caption__text"><p>The Netatmo Welcome camera, left, alongside Netatmo's weather station.</p>
</span><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionCredit-cUgOGk gwmweI hRFzlA caption__credit">Courtesy of Netatmo</span></div></figure><div class="AdWrapper-fGiLaV iGDxYx ad ad--sponsor-product"><div class="ad__slot ad__slot--sponsor-product" data-node-id="25vk4q"></div></div><p><strong>Netatmo Welcome</strong> The French company released its sleek, brushed-bronze security camera last year to much acclaim. Besides looking great, the system incorporates advanced face-recognition technology to alert you when certain members of your family arrive home. When the system hears an alarm (entrance, smoke, or carbon monoxide), it alerts you and automatically records a video; $199. <a data-offer-url="https://www.netatmo.com/product/camera" class="external-link text link" data-event-click="{&quot;element&quot;:&quot;ExternalLink&quot;,&quot;outgoingURL&quot;:&quot;https://www.netatmo.com/product/camera&quot;}" href="https://www.netatmo.com/product/camera" rel="nofollow noopener" target="_blank"><em>netatmo.com</em></a></p><figure class="AssetEmbedWrapper-iJvQnD cOWUYC asset-embed"><div </span><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionCredit-cUgOGk gwmweI hRFzlA caption__credit">WKG Creative, Courtesy of Eero</span></div></figure></div></div></div><div class="GridItem-bufwYC fdedvq grid--item grid-layout__aside"></div></div><div class="GridWrapper-uulHE dCnZHU grid grid-margins grid-items-2 ArticlePageChunksGrid-kSknMg dSvDdx grid-layout--adrail narrow wide-adrail" data-journey-hook="grid-wrapper"><div class="GridItem-bufwYC kKTABR grid--item grid-layout__content"><div class="BodyWrapper-cuqCAc fFudnb body body__container article__body" data-journey-hook="client-content" data-testid="BodyWrapper"><div class="body__inner-container"><p><strong>Eero Router</strong> CEO Nick Weaver founded Eero when he became fed up with a worsening Wi-Fi connection after moving into a larger home. The concept: multiple routers connected to one network, effectively spreading your connectivity farther. The routers are smartphone-controlled, allowing you to set time limits, create unique passwords, and monitor access, all with the swipe of a finger. The compact, minimalist devices are pretty enough to be left out, blending seamlessly into a white space; from $199 for a single device. <a data-offer-url="https://eero.com/" class="external-link text link" data-event-click="{&quot;element&quot;:&quot;ExternalLink&quot;,&quot;outgoingURL&quot;:&quot;https://eero.com/&quot;}" href="https://eero.com/" rel="nofollow noopener" target="_blank"><em>eero.com</em></a></p><figure class="AssetEmbedWrapper-iJvQnD cOWUYC asset-embed"><div class="AssetEmbedAssetContainer-fnduJP iaVSwI asset-embed__asset-container"><span mode.</p>
</span><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionCredit-cUgOGk gwmweI hRFzlA caption__credit">Courtesy of Nest</span></div></figure><p><strong>Nest Learning Thermostat</strong> The sleek thermostat from early smart-home purveyor Nest is a case study in form and function working in tandem. Its round screen lights up in different colors to signify various settings and warnings (orange for heating, blue for cooling, black for an empty home), making it an intuitive system that’s easy to work into your life; $249. <a data-offer-url="https://nest.com/thermostat/meet-nest-thermostat/" class="external-link text link" data-event-click="{&quot;element&quot;:&quot;ExternalLink&quot;,&quot;outgoingURL&quot;:&quot;https://nest.com/thermostat/meet-nest-thermostat/&quot;}" href="https://nest.com/thermostat/meet-nest-thermostat/" rel="nofollow noopener" target="_blank"><em>nest.com</em></a></p><figure class="AssetEmbedWrapper-iJvQnD cOWUYC asset-embed"><div </span><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionCredit-cUgOGk gwmweI hRFzlA caption__credit">Courtesy of Plume</span></div></figure></div></div></div><div class="GridItem-bufwYC fdedvq grid--item grid-layout__aside"></div></div><div class="GridWrapper-uulHE dCnZHU grid grid-margins grid-items-2 ArticlePageChunksGrid-kSknMg dSvDdx grid-layout--adrail narrow wide-adrail" data-journey-hook="grid-wrapper"><div class="GridItem-bufwYC kKTABR grid--item grid-layout__content"><div class="BodyWrapper-cuqCAc fFudnb body body__container article__body" data-journey-hook="client-content" data-testid="BodyWrapper"><div class="body__inner-container"><p><strong>Plume Pod</strong> The Plume’s looks are deceiving. The device stores router technology in a cloud, so the only physical aspect is a small “pod” that gets plugged into your outlet. Such a setup also allows the software to continually self-optimize to bring you the best connection. The tiny device, which starts shipping this fall, comes in onyx, silver, and Champagne faceted designs; $39 per pod. <a data-offer-url="https://www.plumewifi.com/" class="external-link text link" data-event-click="{&quot;element&quot;:&quot;ExternalLink&quot;,&quot;outgoingURL&quot;:&quot;https://www.plumewifi.com/&quot;}" href="https://www.plumewifi.com/" rel="nofollow noopener" Echo</p>
</span><span class="BaseText-fEwdHD CaptionCredit-cUgOGk gwmweI hRFzlA caption__credit">Courtesy of Amazon</span></div></figure><p><strong>Amazon Dot</strong> After the wildly successful introduction of Amazon’s Echo and its Alexa technology, the tech giant will release this fall the Dot, a tiny round device that connects to any speaker and essentially makes it an Echo. So if you’re an audiophile who is intrigued by the Echo’s capability but will never change your Sonos system, the Dot solves both problems. Compact, efficient, and easy to use, it’s ideal for both the tech-savvy and the smarthome uninitiated; $90. <a
